#FDS-001 - Traversal (Player Movement)

12 messages from 4 people

Matt Barnes opened the #FDS-001 Traversal thread for discussing player movement. Skullbuster asked how walking differs from swimming and whether swimming allows 3D movement. Matt explained that major areas will be oxygenated and walkable like the surface. The suit's weighted shoes also let players walk on the sea floor and other underwater surfaces, and players should be able to swim too. MM said walking because of weighted shoes while also swimming felt off. He used his game-jam robot sidescroller as an example of breaking believability and asked whether pointing out such things was useful. Matt replied that the game is a steampunk fantasy ("what does a fantasy work look like to someone in the Victorian era"), grounded in reality so that intentional rule-breaks feel like fantasy. Disagreements should be settled through conversation and compromise.

MM clarified that he meant "believable" rather than realistic. He suggested a heavy suit plus a jetpack, with oxygen used for both breathing and propulsion. Skullbuster also suggested a water-only propulsion pack for 3D movement. Matt said the design is inspired by a "Scaphander" suit with heavy boots. The default state will be sinking until you land on a walkable surface. Planned extra states include spending oxygen to become neutrally buoyant and swim, and spending more to float upward. He'll add this to his documentation, especially the tutorial docs. Propulsion tools are fine for special occasions, but core movement should stay under the player's own power, with no "speeder bike" style travel. MM said the clarification resolved his confusion.

Highlights - Matt Barnes created the thread as the place to discuss the Player Movement feature. - Major areas will be oxygenated and walkable as if on the surface, and weighted shoes allow walking on the sea floor too. - MM questioned how weighted shoes and swimming fit together, citing his game-jam robot sidescroller as an example of believability issues. - Matt framed the game as a steampunk fantasy grounded in reality, asking what fantasy would look like to someone in the Victorian era. - MM proposed oxygen as a shared resource for breathing and propulsion, and Skullbuster suggested a water-only propulsion pack for 3D movement. - Matt's plan: by default the player sinks to a surface, then spends oxygen to become neutrally buoyant to swim or to float up, inspired by the Scaphander suit. - Propulsion tools are limited to special occasions, and primary movement stays under the player's own power (no 'speeder bike').

Topics: Player movement, Walking vs swimming, Buoyancy and oxygen mechanics, Realism vs believability, Propulsion packs, Scaphander suit design

Open questions - Will underwater swimming allow full 3D movement? This was asked by Skullbuster and not explicitly answered. - Will low oxygen force the player to walk only? MM dislikes forced walking, and this wasn't resolved. - On what 'special occasions' would a propulsion pack be available?

Notable messages

I think grounded in realism is an important starting point, but we are not making a realistic game. This is effectively a fantasy game with a steam-punk setting. I would try to frame it in your mind of "what does a fantasy work look like to someone in the Victorian era."

This is where grounded in reality becomes important. Basing things like physics, movement, bio-chemical reactions, etc, in reality helps it feel familiar so that when we intentionally apply something that breaks these rules, it feels like fantasy.

So id say try to build something that you feel comfortable with ensuring it fits into agreed upon boundaries. If you feel strongly about something that doesn't quite fit, we just have to have a conversation with involved parties and come to a compromise we are happy with. A goal of "this just doesn't feel real might be addressed without directly forcing realism.

— Matt Barnes in #FDS-001 - Traversal (Player Movement) (jump)

I think I didn't explain mysefl well enough. We talked about this in the design discussion channel in a more generic way. "Believable" is a better word to describe what I mean. As I was saying in the other thread, I try to challenge every new choice against what I had decided earlier, and adjust the ones that seem off until everything feel cohesive and coherent, or believable if you prefer.

In this case I'd ask: - why do we need to walk underwater? scuba divers can kind of walk on the bottom of the sea, but it doesn't look like a proper walk. - if we need to, how do we make it possible? Heavy shoes wouldn't allow swimming, but maybe a heavy suit and a Jetpack would be more believable despite being even less realistic. Maybe we use oxygen for both breathing and propelling and when low on oxygen we can only walk as proposed in the oxygen thread. I actually still don't like the forced walking because it could be annoying for the player but I like this framing of the idea over the stamina one.

— MM in #FDS-001 - Traversal (Player Movement) (jump)

Thes are much more precise questions and will be updating my documentation to cover that, especially as I approach tutorial documentation.

  1. Why walk on the floor? The direction for the game was to use a "Scaphander" which uses heavy boots allowing you to walk on surfaces under water. Now, we have some flexibility to be inspired by this suit and play with features that suit us.

  2. I am going to initially take an approach where your default state is to sink until you land on something to walk on, but I do think we should have other "states." For example, I would like to make a state where you use some of your available oxygen to make yourself neutrally boyant allowing you to swim, and perhaps utilize even more of your available oxygen to float up. This is a common practice with modern diving, but there's no reason why we can't find a way to mesh the two ideas.

— Matt Barnes in #FDS-001 - Traversal (Player Movement) (jump)

Yes, I do think that this is something that we can encourage, but I would limit it to special occasions or uses. We want the primary movements and control to be under the players power. We can utilize tools for traversal, actions, and combat, but we should not have somehting like "this is my speeder bike I need to travel around."

— Matt Barnes in #FDS-001 - Traversal (Player Movement) (jump)

#Production Drive Backup

10 messages from 1 people

This week in #Production Drive Backup was essentially a solo push by YosephAlex4nder to get the backup plan moving. On Sun Jul 19 he offered to write documentation on how the backup would work by EOD Tuesday if no one else had picked it up. On Wed Jul 22 he told Sidds he agreed with Seth's earlier point that he might be overcomplicating things. Instead of having volunteers run a program, he proposed that they simply download the whole production drive as a single zip file on a set schedule, replacing the old zip each time. He sketched two example rotations: a 2-volunteer setup alternating between 12p GST and 12p PST, and a 12-volunteer setup with downloads every two hours on AEST. He also floated a looser option where people download whenever they 'clock in' and coordinate if something goes wrong.

He decided a schedule was the better route, since volunteers in different time zones would likely overlap. To support that he created a Google Sheets signup and a Google Doc with instructions, then moved the ClickUp task to 'READY FOR QA'. On Thu Jul 23 he said he had looped in David "AssassinCS" Thompson to cover any further work while he is away. No one else posted in the channel this week, so Sidds hasn't yet responded to the proposal.

Highlights - YosephAlex4nder agreed with Seth that the original plan was overcomplicated and proposed volunteers just download the whole drive as one zip on a schedule. - Example rotations were drafted for 2 volunteers (12p GST / 12p PST) and for 12 volunteers (every two hours, AEST). - A volunteer signup Google Sheet and an instructions Google Doc were created. - The ClickUp task was moved to 'READY FOR QA'. - David "AssassinCS" Thompson was looped in to cover follow-up work while YosephAlex4nder is away.

— solomongrundee in #Kyle Solomon - Poet & Writer (jump)

A fellow Superjump Magazine contributor! Good to see you here. 😄

For those who don't know, Kyle's article on Yakuza's "food as function" is an interesting read. It reminded me so much of Tarantino's philosophy about using food to contextualise the character or the scene. https://www.superjumpmagazine.com/eat-like-a-dragon-what-yakuzas-food-reveals-about-character/

I never dabbled into poetry until lately, shortly after I caught sight of, and was inspired by, Nathalie Lawhead's game "she danced in the wind like a holographic dream before the world died". https://alienmelon.itch.io/flower

There's a game jam about game poems happening, if you're thinking of trying to merge the two: https://itch.io/jam/nfts-game-poems-jam

— Pruthvi in #Kyle Solomon - Poet & Writer (jump)

#📊│analytics-lead-discussion

10 messages from 4 people

Felix proposed starting analytics "alignment" next week once the current rush is over, and shadowarrior agreed. shadowarrior then shared the main points from an earlier talk with Shane. The plan is to document how Game Analytics (GA) can hook into the Unreal version the team uses, with help from @VibeBlind's team, and to connect Unreal's built-in analytics data points to GA. The team also needs to decide where the database lives: GA's cloud storage might cost money per events × MAU, much like Amplitude, so a local DB such as SQLite could be an alternative. Once the pipeline works, the next steps are to identify extra events with Seth's team (for example load_start and load_end) and to keep a Google Sheet listing every event ID, what it tracks, its data type and its table. Felix said he would add all of this to the wiki and expects tech, marketing and community to get tasks later.

Later in the week, pulsegenerator asked GrandHawk whether he would take on User Research (UR) and UX testing, since an onboarding group had raised the question. GrandHawk said his focus is on observing user behavior once players have the game, and he sees UR as closer to directing or producing. Both agreed UR probably isn't necessary because the game isn't being monetized. pulsegenerator will run this by Diana, and for now the plan is to do UX testing at some point, which GrandHawk has in his sights.

Highlights - Analytics alignment sync is planned for next week, after the rush. - shadowarrior outlined the plan to integrate Game Analytics with Unreal's built-in analytics, with help from VibeBlind's team. - There is an open concern that GA cloud storage could cost money per events × MAU, with a local SQLite DB as a possible fallback. - The team proposed a Google Sheet event dictionary covering event IDs, what each tracks, data type and table. - Felix is adding the analytics items to the wiki and expects tech, marketing and community tasks to follow. - pulsegenerator and GrandHawk agreed User Research is likely unnecessary since the game isn't monetized; UX testing will go ahead. - pulsegenerator will confirm the UR decision with Diana.

— Taplay in #Tavin Peterson - Narrative Designer and Writer (jump)

Yes, I worked on a project with a team where we made a game that's a retelling of the Fox and the Grapes. Basically, the whole idea of the game is that you can't actually get the grapes on any of the levels, and you can only progress by leaving the level. And you also see that you're trying to get the grapes to feed your cubs. But since you can't get them, they start dying off. We also had the levels get darker and gloomier as you go on. Another fun detail of this is that we had 5 levels, and we used the 5 stages of grief as a loose framework for exactly how you're prevented from getting the grapes in each one.

#Task Workflow

9 messages from 4 people

shadowarrior opened a thread for questions and comments on the new task workflow. They set out ClickUp access rules that Lori had already confirmed with them. All Discipline Leads and Deputy Leads, including Production Deputy Leads, get Admin access. Individual contributors are invited as Limited Members, and the Producer adds them to the specific tasks they're assigned, which limits the project's risk exposure. shadowarrior also pointed out that the workflow diagram was missing a QA slice. They proposed tracking QA bugs in GitHub against each feature's task list, and keeping the ClickUp EPIC from being marked Complete until QA signs off with the Feature owner.

sabishi_angel agreed with the QA loop-back but noted that the RC testplan currently lives as a ClickUp task list, not in GitHub. shadowarrior was fine either way and suggested a GitHub task list under the RC might be easier: if the testplan passes, the RC is a Pass Build. They left the integration decision to the QA folks. pulsegenerator will talk with VibeBlind about where the RC checklist should live, and understands that all bugs will go into GitHub Issues and sync to ClickUp. Cafedunoir added the workflow to the Production wiki on ClickUp.

#📢│audio-announcements

7 messages from 3 people

The audio team got its official kickoff this week. On Jul 20, Hailey (introducing herself as Bori, the Audio Co-Lead / Producer) welcomed the new @Audio members and shared a welcome video from Audio Lead James (exackley). She also posted the onboarding basics: a sign-up sheet where members list their comfortable disciplines (sound design, voice, implementation or music) and a Google Drive folder with the team's visions, ideas and structure. On tools, implementation will be done in MetaSounds with no middleware. Everyone can use whatever DAW and plugins they like, since DAW session files won't be shared.

Hailey then posted a when2meet poll to find a time for the first sync-up. James noted that Friday 12pm PST was the only slot that worked for everyone, so Hailey booked the meeting and shared a Google Calendar invite. Jorge Daniel also shared the project's public ClickUp wiki and suggested starting with the Audio Wiki page and then exploring other disciplines. James was glad the wiki is finally working and shareable.

Highlights - Hailey (Bori), Audio Co-Lead / Producer, welcomed the team and shared a welcome video from Audio Lead James Ackley. - New members were asked to fill out a sheet listing which disciplines they're comfortable with: sound design, voice, implementation or music. - Implementation will use MetaSounds with no middleware, and members can use any DAW and plugins they prefer. - A Google Drive folder with the team's visions, ideas and structure was shared, and everyone is invited to add their own ideas. - The first audio sync-up is set for Friday at 12pm PST, the only time everyone was available, and a calendar invite went out. - Jorge Daniel shared the public ClickUp wiki and recommended starting with the Audio Wiki page.

Topics: Onboarding, Team introductions, Audio tools (MetaSounds, DAWs), Meeting scheduling, Project wiki

Notable messages

Hello @Audio folks, welcome to the team! I’m Bori, the Audio Co-Lead / Producer. And, a warm greeting from our Audio Lead, James!

https://youtu.be/Vsaz3f8i0Lk

Here is some onboarding information:

  • Disciplines: Please let us know which audio disciplines you are comfortable with: sound design, voice, implementation, or music. Please fill out this sheet so we can get to know you better. https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/17lCWiGd8ffK5phkuDIJH_NkHO5g6k_b3YobTuAs7OH4/edit?gid=0#gid=0

  • Tools: We will use MetaSounds for implementation, and no middleware will be used. For DAWs and plugins, you may use the tools you are most comfortable with. Since we will not be sharing DAW session files, feel free to create your sounds using your preferred setup.

  • Ideas and structure: Here are some of the visions, ideas, and structures we have been discussing. Please feel free to share your ideas as well. https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1MTpFklTbeDjNkEgMxUZjKRw1CKx4Xa4J

#FDS-006 - Journal Feature

6 messages from 4 people

The #FDS-006 Journal Feature channel brainstormed what the in-game journal should look and feel like. After Matt Barnes pinged Glenny, MM pointed out that the journal can be used underwater. MM suggested a retro digital device, like the one from Fallout, alongside a paper journal. The paper journal would hold notes and drawings, while the digital device would hold organized information and double as a menu/UI for things like oxygen level.

Matt Barnes called this a good starting point and said it would be refined as other departments' PoCs come together. Glenny built on it with a two-in-one design: a tablet cover showing basic info for the districts and underwater, with pages inside for clues and story. Later in the week, Henry, after reading the core GDD, suggested a charcoal-rubbing mini-game inspired by Uncharted: Golden Abyss (PS Vita) for journal discoveries and possibly puzzles, adapted for mouse input. Nothing was formally decided beyond adopting MM's idea as a starting point.

Highlights - MM proposed a Fallout-style retro digital device paired with a paper journal, with the digital side also acting as a UI for oxygen level and similar stats. - Matt Barnes endorsed MM's idea as a starting point, to be refined against other departments' PoCs. - Glenny suggested a two-in-one journal: a tablet cover for district and underwater info, with inner pages for clues and story. - Henry pitched a charcoal-rubbing mini-game, inspired by Uncharted: Golden Abyss, for journal discoveries and puzzles using the mouse.

#Implementation Ownership?

5 messages from 3 people

Jorge Daniel raised a question from the onboarding sessions: will a dedicated person handle implementation handoff, or will each team member implement their own work? Daniel Beahn asked for clarification, since "implementation" means different things to different people. Jorge explained that it likely came from one of the 3D artists asking about asset delivery: do contributors put their own assets into the engine, or does someone receive and implement them?

Jorge then tagged the Art Lead to take the question. Zef replied with his current understanding: Art will not put its own assets in game. Finished assets get committed to the drive, and then design, tech, or whoever needs the asset places it properly so nothing breaks. Nobody formally confirmed this, and the Art Lead hadn't responded by the end of the week.

Highlights - Jorge Daniel brought an onboarding question about who owns implementation handoff. - Daniel Beahn asked for context because "implementation" gets used in many different ways. - Jorge clarified that the question came from a 3D artist asking about asset delivery into the engine. - Jorge tagged the Art Lead to answer the question from the onboarding sessions. - Zef shared his understanding: artists commit finished assets to the drive, and design, tech, or whoever needs them puts them in game so nothing breaks.

Topics: Asset delivery pipeline, Implementation ownership, Onboarding questions, Art and tech handoff

Open questions - The Art Lead has not officially confirmed whether artists or a dedicated person/team implements assets in the engine. - Will there be one dedicated person for implementation handoff, or does it fall to whichever discipline (design, tech) needs the asset?

Notable messages

So far the way I understand it, Art will not be handling putting their own assets in game. They will be committed to the drive when complete and then design, tech, or whoever needs the asset will be putting it in its proper place so that nothing breaks

Do we know who asked this? Implementation gets used a lot of different ways by different people, I'd like to understand the question better before trying to answer.

#I want to write a TDD!!!

5 messages from 1 people

Swart Tigerhorn kicked off the #I want to write a TDD!!! thread on Fri Jul 24 as the starting point for the project's Tech Design work. Members who aren't interested in tech design can safely skip it for now. Swart asked interested folks to first read the Project Structure page and then the TDD Template, shared for now as a PDF (TDD_-_Unreal_Blueprints.pdf). The Game Designers are already working on their documents, and the team plans to start its very first TDD early next week.

Swart gave a personal recommendation: whoever writes a TDD should not be the one who implements that feature in Unreal. Having someone with fresh eyes build from the document is a natural way to review it, learn how to write better ones, and is good practice overall. Swart asked people to react with 👍 if they'd like to write the first TDD, just to gauge interest for now; it got one reaction. Swart also invited questions in the thread. Nothing was decided yet beyond the plan and the call for volunteers.

Highlights - Swart Tigerhorn opened a dedicated thread for the Tech Design side of the project, noting that people not interested can skip it for now. - The TDD Template for Unreal Blueprints was shared as a PDF (TDD_-_Unreal_Blueprints.pdf), to be read after the Project Structure page. - Game Designers are already working on their documents, and the first TDD is planned for early next week. - Swart recommended that a TDD's author should not implement that same feature in Unreal, so a fresh set of eyes effectively reviews the document. - Swart ran an interest check (react 👍 to volunteer to write the first TDD), which got one reaction so far. - Swart opened the floor for questions in the thread.

Topics: Technical Design Documents (TDD), Unreal Blueprints, TDD template, Project structure, Volunteering / interest check, Review practices

Open questions - Who will write the first TDD? Interest is still being gauged, with one 👍 reaction so far. - Which feature the first TDD will cover hasn't been specified yet. - Swart invited questions in the thread, but none were posted yet.

Notable messages

After reading the documents, let me know if writing one of these is of interest for you.

Keep in mind, as a personal recommendation, whoever get to write the TDD should not be the one to implement that same feature in Unreal.

Because having someone with a fresh set of eyes trying to work based on your document is a great way of getting it reviewed, learning how to make it better next time, and all around good practice.

React to this message with 👍 if you would like to write the first TDD. (Just to gauge interest for now)

— Swart Tigerhorn in #I want to write a TDD!!! (jump)

#Important info for the jam - Pods

4 messages from 2 people

This week's #Important info for the jam - Pods channel held Onboarding Step 7, which explains pods. Michal Fried defined a pod as a multi-disciplinary group built around delivering one specific feature, so members can work together on the same mission at once. Pods are organized by the Discipline Leads and assigned to a deputy lead. GreenCat then shared a video (Alex_Video_2_Game_Jam.mp4) in which Xelnath explains how the team works together, how decisions are made, and what to do if life happens.

Michal Fried also reassured newcomers that once they pick a department, their department team members, leads and the Project team will support them throughout the game jam. That includes walking them through taking on new tasks, contributing to their department and settling in over the next couple of weeks. The post ends with links to the next and previous onboarding steps and a Common Questions thread, plus a note that unanswered questions should go to the designated questions channel. Nothing was decided here; the channel is informational.

One of the most important things in the project is the concepts of pods

A pod is a multi-disciplinary group, focused around delivering a specific feature. This allows you to focus on working together on the same mission at once. Pods are organized by the Discipline Leads, and are assigned to a deputy lead.

#Art Resource Sharing

4 messages from 1 people

Smetch_D kicked off the new #Art Resource Sharing channel on Mon Jul 20 as a place for the art team to share personal resources, links and tips. They posted three resources that day.

The first was Material Maker (https://www.materialmaker.org/), a free alternative to Substance Designer that they've been enjoying. The second was Michael Vicente's "tried and true" Orb brush pack for Blender 5.0, attached as a zip. It came with step-by-step install instructions: append each brush from the sculpting tab, then save via File > Defaults > Save Startup File so the brushes stick around. The third was a search tip for cutting AI images out of results by adding exclusion terms like -"stable diffusion" -"ai" -"midjourney". No one else posted, so there were no decisions or discussion.

Highlights - Smetch_D opened the channel for the art team to share personal resources, links and tips. - Material Maker (materialmaker.org) was recommended as a free alternative to Substance Designer. - Michael Vicente's Orb brush pack for Blender 5.0 was shared as a zip, with install steps (+2 reactions). - Key Blender step: after appending the brushes, use File > Defaults > Save Startup File so they load every time. - Smetch_D hasn't found a way to append all the Orb brushes at once and noted that other extraction options exist. - Search tip: add -"stable diffusion" -"ai" -"midjourney" to image searches to filter out much of the AI content (not foolproof).

Topics: Channel kickoff, Material Maker, Blender sculpting brushes, Orb brush pack, Image search tips, Filtering AI images

Open questions - Is there a way to append all the Orb brushes into Blender at once instead of one by one?

Notable messages

The tried and true orb Blender brush pack by Michael Vicente. To access: extract file anywhere > open Blender as NEW FILE, don't save > go to sculpting tab > File > Append... > find the unzipped brush folder > click into each file until you get to the brush > click on brush > append, the brush should pop up in your brushes bar > IMPORTANT LAST STEPS!!! once all brushed are added, go to File > Defaults > Save Startup File. This will save the brushes next time you open Blender. Happy sculpting!!! (Haven't found a way to append them all at once. 😓 Other extraction options exist!)

— Smetch_D in #Art Resource Sharing (jump)

TIP: Decluttering AI results!!! When using search engines for browsing images, adding -ai terms remove a lot of ai from the results, like: -"stable diffusion" -"ai" -"midjourney" Not foolproof, but helps.

— Smetch_D in #Art Resource Sharing (jump)

Images - Michael Vicente's Orb brush pack for Blender 5.0, a sculpting staple shared by Smetch_D.

#ADD Reference 🧵

4 messages from 1 people

In a short burst of messages on Friday, Clara shared an audio design doc (Scension_Audio_Design_Doc.pdf) from an old project whose owner ghosted all the devs, offering it as a reference. She explained that the doc's core idea is the Audio Pillars: three "flowery" words that help orient developers without being very technical. She also defined "Rip-o-matic" as a term for content you use as a reference and rip ideas from. She noted the doc is missing a LUFS guidance section, which would be good to add.

Clara then posted a rough audio design doc template with three sections. Vision Statement holds the emotional design description and flowery language that guides asset creators. Technical Design breaks the work into design-goal bullets with detailed technical sub-bullets explaining how asset definitions are used. Event Design lists the required events, including Create, Play, Stop and Destroy. She called it a really rough template that captures the doc's core identity. No decisions were made; this was a resource share.

Vision Statement - The emotional design description goes here. - Any flowery language should go here and guide asset creators in the overall direction Technical Design - Break these up into design goal bullet points and write up technical design description sub-bullets for each design goal. Go into painstaking detail and explain how you use the asset definitions. Event Design - Break down the necessary events that will be required for this feature. This includes Create, Play, Stop, and Destroy events.

#What are the minimal tech requirements for Unreal?

3 messages from 2 people

A short exchange in the thread about minimal tech requirements for Unreal Engine. hollow pinged VibeBlind, who shared a link to Epic's official 'Hardware and Software Specifications for Unreal Engine' page from the Unreal Engine 5.7 documentation. hollow then posted the key specs: Windows 10 64-bit (version 1909 revision .1350 or higher, or versions 2004/20H2 revision .789 or higher), with Windows 11 compatible with UE5 and within the recommended specs. The rest was a quad-core Intel or AMD processor at 2.5 GHz or faster, 32 GB RAM, 8 GB or more of graphics RAM, and a DirectX 11 or 12 compatible graphics card with the latest drivers.

No further discussion followed. The thread's question was answered by pointing to the official documentation and its listed specs.

Highlights - VibeBlind shared Epic's official UE 5.7 hardware and software specifications documentation link. - hollow summarized the specs: Windows 10 64-bit (1909 rev .1350+ or 2004/20H2 rev .789+), and noted Windows 11 is compatible with UE5. - Listed specs include a quad-core Intel/AMD CPU at 2.5 GHz+, 32 GB RAM, 8 GB+ VRAM, and a DirectX 11/12 compatible GPU with the latest drivers.

Topics: Unreal Engine 5 system requirements, Hardware specs, Windows compatibility, UE 5.7 documentation

Notable messages

Operating System

Windows 10 64-bit version 1909 revision .1350 or higher, or versions 2004 and 20H2 revision .789 or higher.

Windows 11 is compatible with UE5 and fits in the recommended specs.

Processor: Quad-core Intel or AMD, 2.5 GHz or faster

Memory:32 GB RAM

Graphics RAM:8 GB or more

Graphics Card: DirectX 11 or 12 compatible graphics card with the latest drivers.

— hollow in #What are the minimal tech requirements for Unreal? (jump)

— VibeBlind in #What are the minimal tech requirements for Unreal? (jump)

#🍎mentor-start-here🍎

3 messages from 1 people

This week, Michal Fried posted the welcome and onboarding guide for new Mentors, Coaches, and Teachers joining ASGC's first-ever Community Game Jam. The message frames the role simply: mentors aren't there to manage teams or ship a game, they're there to help people grow, since many participants are new to game jams, trying new disciplines, or working on large collaborative teams for the first time. Mentors are asked to read the #📑│faq channel, especially the Mentor FAQ, which explains what a Mentor is, how the role differs from a Discipline Lead, and how mentorship fits into the jam's structure. In the discipline-specific mentor channels, mentors will answer questions, help newcomers, share tutorials and resources, offer career advice, give optional feedback when teams ask for it, and help keep things welcoming.

The guide also sets boundaries with Discipline Leads. Leads own production, priorities, scheduling, and team direction. Mentors should not direct production work or override a Lead's decisions, and should act as trusted advisors rather than another layer of management. Mentors are also asked to be a second set of eyes: if someone seems stuck, discouraged, in conflict, or has concerns worth escalating, they should flag it to a Discipline Lead or the Production Team early. Michal closed with navigation links to the previous and next onboarding steps. There is a placeholder 'Common Questions: PLEASE ADD' section, and further questions should go to the designated questions channel.

Highlights - Michal Fried welcomed new Mentors, Coaches, and Teachers to ASGC's first-ever Community Game Jam. - The role was summed up as: 'Leads build the game. Mentors help build the people.' - Mentors are asked to read the #📑│faq channel, especially the Mentor FAQ, before starting. - Mentor duties include answering questions, sharing resources, giving career insights, and offering optional feedback in discipline-specific mentor channels. - Mentors should not direct production or override Discipline Leads; they act as trusted advisors, not another layer of management. - Mentors should escalate struggling participants, conflicts, or behavior concerns to a Discipline Lead or the Production Team early. - The jam's stated goal is learning and trying new things, not winning.

Topics: Mentor onboarding, ASGC Community Game Jam, Mentor vs Discipline Lead roles, Escalation and participant support, Mentor FAQ

Open questions - The 'Common Questions' section in the onboarding post still says 'PLEASE ADD' and has no content yet.

Notable messages

🎓 Welcome to the Mentor / Coach / Teacher Team!

First off... thank you.

We're excited to welcome all of our new Mentors, Coaches, and Teachers to ASGC's first-ever Community Game Jam.

You aren't here to manage a team or ship a game. You're here to help people grow.

Many participants are joining their very first game jam, trying a new discipline, or working with large collaborative teams for the first time. Your experience can make all the difference in helping someone build confidence, learn something new, and have an amazing experience.

📖 First Things First

Please take a few minutes to review the #📑│faq channel, especially the Mentor FAQ.

It explains:

What a Mentor is The difference between a Mentor and a Discipline Lead How mentorship fits into the overall structure of the Game Jam

The short version:

Leads build the game. Mentors help build the people.

🎯 Where You'll Be Helping

You'll have access to the discipline-specific mentor channels where you can:

• Answer questions from participants • Help newcomers who may be learning a discipline for the first time • Share tutorials, documentation, videos, articles, and other helpful resources • Offer career advice or industry insights when appropriate • Provide optional feedback when teams ask for another perspective • Help create a welcoming learning environment

🎤 Learning & Development

One of our biggest goals is to create an ongoing learning experience throughout the Game Jam.

We'd love your help by:

• Hosting workshops or live sessions • Running office hours or AMA discussions • Inviting industry professionals to speak • Volunteering to present on a topic you're passionate about • Recording short educational videos or tutorials for our learning archive

If you have an idea, we'd love to hear it.

— Michal Fried in #🍎mentor-start-here🍎 (jump)

🤝 Working Alongside Discipline Leads

Discipline Leads are responsible for production, priorities, scheduling, and team direction.

Mentors are here to educate, encourage, and support.

Please avoid directing production work or overriding decisions made by a Discipline Lead. If a team asks for feedback, offer guidance and best practices while respecting the Lead's ownership of the project.

Think of yourself as a trusted advisor, not another layer of management.

👀 **Be a Second Set of Eyes ** As mentors, you'll often interact with participants who may be struggling before anyone else notices.

If you see someone who:

Seems stuck or discouraged Needs additional support Has concerns that should be escalated Is experiencing conflict or behavior that should be brought to leadership

Please let a Discipline Lead or the Production Team know so we can help early.

🌱 Our Goal

This Game Jam isn't about winning.

It's about learning.

It's about trying something new.

It's about helping people discover skills they didn't know they had.

Thank you for volunteering your time, knowledge, and experience to make that happen.

We're incredibly excited to have you aboard. Let's help build an unforgettable learning experience together.

— Michal Fried in #🍎mentor-start-here🍎 (jump)

#Jhoshua Ampo [PST]

3 messages from 2 people

Jhoshua Ampo (WaffleFish) introduced himself in his channel: a recent Computer Science graduate aiming to make a living from games, with 20+ game jams under his belt (3 days to 2 months long) and experience leading a team that took A Slice of Lunch from scratch to a Steam release, all in Unity/C#. His Unreal experience is one small team project touching player controls and AI agents a couple of years ago, and his C++ is rusty, so he sees this project as a timely chance to grow in Unreal and C++. He also shared that he's into Slay the Spire 2, Marvel Rivals, archipelago randomized speed runs and DMing DnD 5e, plus his LinkedIn and itch.io links. The intro got 20+ reactions.

mugi, a fellow recent grad, welcomed him and asked how archipelago runs are organized. Jhoshua replied with a five-step walkthrough: mod your game to connect to archipelago, create yaml settings files, generate a zipped seed file with their program, upload it to the website to host a lobby, then connect and play. He pointed to archipelago.gg (and its Discord) and a community Google Sheet listing many more supported games.

Highlights - WaffleFish (Jhoshua) introduced himself as a recent CS grad with 20+ game jams and a Steam-published team game, A Slice of Lunch, built in Unity/C#. - He's looking to expand into Unreal and C++, building on one earlier small Unreal project covering player controls and AI agents. - His intro drew 20+ reactions and included his LinkedIn and itch.io (wafflefish.itch.io) links. - mugi welcomed him as a fellow recent graduate and asked how archipelago randomized speed runs work. - Jhoshua gave a five-step guide to hosting archipelago runs and linked archipelago.gg plus a spreadsheet of community-supported games.

Topics: Introductions, Unity to Unreal transition, C++, Game jams, Archipelago randomizer, DnD 5e

Notable messages

Hello! My name's Jhoshua. My background is that I'm a recent graduate with a Bachelors in Computer Science trying to make a living from making games. I've done over 20 game jams that can span from 3 days to 2 months. I even led a team in developing a game from scratch to publishing it on Steam, called A Slice of Lunch. However, all of those games were made with Unity and C#.

In terms of Unreal experience, I have worked on one small project with a small team. Touching on various concepts like player controls and AI agents. Though it's been a couple of years since I last worked on that project. For C++, I know the basics of how it all works, but it's been a while and I'm a bit rusty with it. This project came at a good time since I've been wanting to work with Unreal and C++ recently and to further expand my skillsets.

Here's some personal stuff to get to know me better. My current favorite games are Slay the Spire 2 and Marvel Rivals. I've been really into archipelago randomized speed runs. (In short it's where you and your buddies play different games, doing certain checks to send each other items in order to complete your game. I.e. beating a boss in Hollow Knight can give the Super Mario 64 triple-jump). I also occasionally dm one shots or campaigns with my friends in DnD 5e.

Here's my socials if you're interested: Linkedin: https://www.linkedin.com/in/jhoshua-ampo/ itchio: https://wafflefish.itch.io/

— WaffleFish in #Jhoshua Ampo [PST] (jump)

It's so much fun to play with friends! If you go to their website they have some really great tutorials on how to set up and host your own archipelago lobbies.

In short how it all works is . . . 1. You mod your game to make it be able to connect to archipelago 2. You and your friends create yaml files to determine the settings of your game like what the goal is for your game and how random you want your game to be 3. You then use their program to generate a zipped file that determines where everyone's items are located 4. You then upload that file to their website in order to host a lobby 5. Finally you connect to that lobby with your game and you're set to play!

That's a brief explanation of it, there are much better tutorials out there that go into more detail or even tutorials specifically for that game you want to play. Cause each game has their own way of modding it to connect to the archipelago server.

Here's a link to their website: https://archipelago.gg/ The website has a link to their discord and I highly suggest joining that too! The website also has a list of games they officially support but here's a much bigger list of games that are also supported for playing archipelago: https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1iuzDTOAvdoNe8Ne8i461qGNucg5OuEoF-Ikqs8aUQZw/htmlview?usp=drivesdk&pru=AAABnDVTqNM*d5Bz0tM64wYoH26DCANQDA#gid=58422002 I hope this helps and I hope you have a fun time trying it out!

— WaffleFish in #Jhoshua Ampo [PST] (jump)
